Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

The secret organization AiZu AnalyticS has launched a top-secret investigation. There are N people targeted, with identification numbers from 1 to N. As an AZAS Information Strategy Investigator, you have decided to determine the number of people in your target who meet at least one of the following conditions:

* Those who do not belong to the organization $ A $ and who own the product $ C $.
* A person who belongs to the organization $ B $ and owns the product $ C $.



A program that calculates the number of people who meet the conditions when the identification number of the person who belongs to the organization $ A $, the person who belongs to the organization $ B $, and the person who owns the product $ C $ is given as input. Create. However, be careful not to count duplicate people who meet both conditions.

(Supplement: Regarding the above conditions)
Let $ A $, $ B $, and $ C $ be the sets of some elements selected from the set of natural numbers from 1 to $ N $. The number of people who satisfy the condition is the number of elements that satisfy $ (\ bar {A} \ cap C) \ cup (B \ cap C) $ (painted part in the figure). However, $ \ bar {A} $ is a complement of the set $ A $.

<image>



Input

The input is given in the following format.


N
X a1 a2 ... aX
Y b1 b2 ... bY
Z c1 c2 ... cZ


The input is 4 lines, and the number of people to be surveyed N (1 ≤ N ≤ 100) is given in the first line. On the second line, the number X (0 ≤ X ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ A $, followed by the identification number ai (1 ≤ ai ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ A $. Given. On the third line, the number Y (0 ≤ Y ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ B $, followed by the identification number bi (1 ≤ bi ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ B $. Given. On the fourth line, the number Z (0 ≤ Z ≤ N) of the person who owns the product $ C $, followed by the identification number ci (1 ≤ ci ≤ N) of the person who owns the product $ C $. ) Is given.

Output

Output the number of people who meet the conditions on one line.

Examples

Input

5
3 1 2 3
2 4 5
2 3 4


Output

1


Input

100
3 1 100 4
0
2 2 3


Output

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. People who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. People who belong to organization B AND own product C

Mathematically, this is: `(¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)`

Where:
- ¬A means "not in A" (complement of set A)
- C means "owns product C"
- ∪ means "union" (at least one condition)
- ∩ means "intersection" (both conditions)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input
- Read N (total number of people)
- Read X and list of people in organization A
- Read Y and list of people in organization B  
- Read Z and list of people who own product C

### Step 2: Convert inputs to sets
- Convert the lists to sets for easier set operations
- Set A: people belonging to organization A
- Set B: people belonging to organization B  
- Set C: people who own product C

### Step 3: Calculate the required condition
We need `(¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)`

This can be broken down as:
- First part: people not in A AND in C = (universal set - A) ∩ C
- Second part: people in B AND in C = B ∩ C
- Union of both parts

We can simplify this using set operations in Python.

### Step 4: Implementation approach
1. Create set A from the given numbers
2. Create set B from the given numbers  
3. Create set C from the given numbers
4. Calculate (set of all people not in A) ∩ C
5. Calculate B ∩ C
6. Take the union of these two sets
7. Return the size of the resulting set

## Let's trace through Example 1:
- N = 5
- A = {1, 2, 3}
- B = {4, 5}
- C = {3, 4}

Group 1: Not in A AND in C
- Not in A: {4, 5}
- Not in A ∩ C: {4} (since 4 is in both)

Group 2: In B AND in C
- B ∩ C: {4} (since 4 is in both)

Union: {4} ∪ {4} = {4}
Size: 1 ✓

This matches the expected output.

## Example 2:
- N = 100
- A = {1, 4, 100}
- B = {} (empty)
- C = {2, 3}

Group 1: Not in A AND in C
- Not in A: {2, 3, 5, 6, ..., 99}
- Not in A ∩ C: {2, 3}

Group 2: In B AND in C
- B ∩ C: {} (empty)

Union: {2, 3} ∪ {} = {2, 3}
Size: 2 ✓

This matches the expected output.

```python
# Read input
N = int(input())
line_A = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_A[0]
set_A = set(line_A[1:]) if X > 0 else set()

line_B = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_B[0]
set_B = set(line_B[1:]) if Y > 0 else set()

line_C = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_C[0]
set_C = set(line_C[1:]) if Z > 0 else set()

# Calculate the number of people who meet at least one condition
# Condition 1: not in A and in C = (universal set - A) ∩ C
# Condition 2: in B and in C = B ∩ C
# Union of both conditions

# People not in A (from 1 to N)
not_A = set(range(1, N + 1)) - set_A

# First condition: not in A and in C
condition_1 = not_A.intersection(set_C)

# Second condition: in B and in C
condition_2 = set_B.intersection(set_C)

# Union of both conditions
result = condition_1.union(condition_2)

# Output the number of people
print(len(result))
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. People who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. People who belong to organization B AND own product C

This is essentially the union of two sets: (¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Analyze the conditions
- Condition 1: People not in A AND in C = (complement of A) ∩ C
- Condition 2: People in B AND in C = B ∩ C
- We want the union of these two sets to avoid counting duplicates

### Step 2: Convert input to sets
- Read N (total people from 1 to N)
- Read set A (people belonging to organization A)
- Read set B (people belonging to organization B) 
- Read set C (people owning product C)

### Step 3: Calculate the required sets
- Find people not in A: complement of A (relative to 1 to N)
- Find people in both (¬A ∩ C) and (B ∩ C)
- Take the union of these two sets

### Step 4: Count the elements in the union

Let me trace through the first example:
- N = 5
- A = {1, 2, 3} (people 1, 2, 3 belong to A)
- B = {4, 5} (people 4, 5 belong to B)
- C = {3, 4} (people 3, 4 own product C)

Condition 1: NOT in A AND in C = {4, 5} - {1, 2, 3} ∩ {3, 4} = {4} (only person 4 is not in A and owns C)
Condition 2: In B AND in C = {4, 5} ∩ {3, 4} = {4} (only person 4 is in both B and C)

Union: {4} ∪ {4} = {4}
Count: 1 ✓

This matches the expected output.

### Step 5: Implementation

```python
# Read input
N = int(input())

# Read organization A
line_a = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_a[0]
if X > 0:
    A = set(line_a[1:])
else:
    A = set()

# Read organization B
line_b = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_b[0]
if Y > 0:
    B = set(line_b[1:])
else:
    B = set()

# Read product C
line_c = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_c[0]
if Z > 0:
    C = set(line_c[1:])
else:
    C = set()

# Calculate condition 1: not in A and in C
not_A = set(range(1, N + 1)) - A
condition1 = not_A.intersection(C)

# Calculate condition 2: in B and in C
condition2 = B.intersection(C)

# Take union of both conditions
result = condition1.union(condition2)

# Output the count
print(len(result))
```

The solution works as follows:
1. Parse the input to get sets A, B, and C
2. Calculate the complement of A (people not in A) using set difference with range(1, N+1)
3. Find intersection of (not A) with C for condition 1
4. Find intersection of B with C for condition 2
5. Take the union of both sets to avoid duplicates
6. Output the size of the union set

This correctly handles all edge cases including empty sets and overlapping conditions.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. People who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. People who belong to organization B AND own product C

This is mathematically represented as: (¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)

Where:
- ¬A is the complement of set A (people not in A)
- ∩ means intersection (common elements)
- ∪ means union (combined elements)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input
- Read N (total people from 1 to N)
- Read X and list of people in organization A
- Read Y and list of people in organization B  
- Read Z and list of people who own product C

### Step 2: Convert to sets for easier operations
- Convert the lists to sets for efficient set operations
- A = set of people in organization A
- B = set of people in organization B  
- C = set of people who own product C

### Step 3: Apply the conditions
- Condition 1: People not in A AND in C = (universal set - A) ∩ C
- Condition 2: People in B AND in C = B ∩ C
- Total people = (Condition 1) ∪ (Condition 2)

### Step 4: Calculate the union
- Use set union to combine both conditions
- Since we're using sets, duplicates are automatically handled

### Step 5: Output the result

Let me trace through the first example:
- N = 5
- A: [1,2,3] → set A = {1,2,3}
- B: [4,5] → set B = {4,5}
- C: [3,4] → set C = {3,4}

Condition 1: Not in A AND in C
- Not in A = {4,5}
- Not in A ∩ C = {4,5} ∩ {3,4} = {4}

Condition 2: In B AND in C
- B ∩ C = {4,5} ∩ {3,4} = {4}

Union: {4} ∪ {4} = {4}
Count: 1 ✓

This matches the expected output.

else:
    B = set()

# Read product C owners
line_c = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_c[0]
if Z > 0:
    C = set(line_c[1:])
else:
    C = set()

# Calculate condition 1: not in A AND in C
not_A = set(range(1, N + 1)) - A
condition1 = not_A.intersection(C)

# Calculate condition 2: in B AND in C
condition2 = B.intersection(C)

# Union of both conditions
result = condition1.union(condition2)

# Output the count
print(len(result))

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

The secret organization AiZu AnalyticS has launched a top-secret investigation. There are N people targeted, with identification numbers from 1 to N. As an AZAS Information Strategy Investigator, you have decided to determine the number of people in your target who meet at least one of the following conditions:

* Those who do not belong to the organization $ A $ and who own the product $ C $.
* A person who belongs to the organization $ B $ and owns the product $ C $.



A program that calculates the number of people who meet the conditions when the identification number of the person who belongs to the organization $ A $, the person who belongs to the organization $ B $, and the person who owns the product $ C $ is given as input. Create. However, be careful not to count duplicate people who meet both conditions.

(Supplement: Regarding the above conditions)
Let $ A $, $ B $, and $ C $ be the sets of some elements selected from the set of natural numbers from 1 to $ N $. The number of people who satisfy the condition is the number of elements that satisfy $ (\ bar {A} \ cap C) \ cup (B \ cap C) $ (painted part in the figure). However, $ \ bar {A} $ is a complement of the set $ A $.

<image>

The input is given in the following format.


N
X a1 a2 ... aX
Y b1 b2 ... bY
Z c1 c2 ... cZ


The input is 4 lines, and the number of people to be surveyed N (1 ≤ N ≤ 100) is given in the first line. On the second line, the number X (0 ≤ X ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ A $, followed by the identification number ai (1 ≤ ai ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ A $. Given. On the third line, the number Y (0 ≤ Y ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ B $, followed by the identification number bi (1 ≤ bi ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ B $. Given. On the fourth line, the number Z (0 ≤ Z ≤ N) of the person who owns the product $ C $, followed by the identification number ci (1 ≤ ci ≤ N) of the person who owns the product $ C $. ) Is given.

Output

Output the number of people who meet the conditions on one line.

Examples

Input

5
3 1 2 3
2 4 5
2 3 4


Output

1


Input

100
3 1 100 4
0
2 2 3

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. People who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. People who belong to organization B AND own product C

This is essentially finding the size of the union of two sets: (¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input
- Read N (total number of people)
- Read organization A members
- Read organization B members  
- Read product C owners

### Step 2: Identify the two conditions
- Condition 1: People not in A AND in C → (¬A ∩ C)
- Condition 2: People in B AND in C → (B ∩ C)

### Step 3: Find the union of both conditions
- We need to avoid double counting people who satisfy both conditions
- So we find the combined set of people who satisfy either condition

### Step 4: Count the total number of unique people

Let me trace through the examples:

**Example 1:**
- N = 5
- A = {1, 2, 3}
- B = {4, 5}
- C = {3, 4}

Condition 1: Not in A AND in C
- Not in A: {4, 5}
- Not in A AND in C: {4} (since 4 is in C and not in A)
- 3 is in A, so not included

Condition 2: In B AND in C
- In B: {4, 5}
- In B AND in C: {4} (since 4 is in both B and C)

Union: {4}
Count: 1 ✓

**Example 2:**
- N = 100
- A = {1, 4, 100}
- B = {} (empty)
- C = {2, 3}

Condition 1: Not in A AND in C
- Not in A: {2, 3, 5, 6, ... , 99}
- Not in A AND in C: {2, 3}

Condition 2: In B AND in C
- In B: {} (empty)
- In B AND in C: {} (empty)

Union: {2, 3}
Count: 2 ✓

## Implementation

```python
# Read input
N = int(input())

# Read organization A members
line_A = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_A[0]
A = set(line_A[1:]) if X > 0 else set()

# Read organization B members
line_B = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_B[0]
B = set(line_B[1:]) if Y > 0 else set()

# Read product C owners
line_C = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_C[0]
C = set(line_C[1:]) if Z > 0 else set()

# Condition 1: Not in A AND in C
not_A_and_C = set()
for person in C:
    if person not in A:
        not_A_and_C.add(person)

# Condition 2: In B AND in C
in_B_and_C = set()
for person in C:
    if person in B:
        in_B_and_C.add(person)

# Union of both conditions
result = not_A_and_C.union(in_B_and_C)

# Output the count
print(len(result))
```

The solution works as follows:

1. Parse all input sets (A, B, C)
2. For condition 1 (¬A ∩ C): iterate through people who own C and check if they're not in A
3. For condition 2 (B ∩ C): iterate through people who own C and check if they're in B  
4. Take the union of both sets to avoid double counting
5. Return the size of the resulting set

Time complexity: O(N) where N is the total number of people
Space complexity: O(N) for storing the sets

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. Those who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. Those who belong to organization B AND own product C

Mathematically, this is: (¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)

This means we need people who:
- Are not in A AND are in C, OR
- Are in B AND are in C

We must avoid counting duplicates (people who satisfy both conditions).

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Identify the sets
- Set A: people belonging to organization A
- Set B: people belonging to organization B  
- Set C: people who own product C

### Step 2: Translate conditions
Condition 1: (¬A ∩ C) = people not in A AND in C
Condition 2: (B ∩ C) = people in B AND in C

We want the union of these two sets: (¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)

### Step 3: Algorithm
1. Read input values
2. Create sets for A, B, and C
3. Find people in (¬A ∩ C): those who are in C but not in A
4. Find people in (B ∩ C): those who are in B and in C
5. Combine both sets (union) and count the total number of unique people

### Step 4: Implementation considerations
- Use Python sets for efficient operations
- The union of two sets automatically handles duplicates
- We need to be careful with empty sets (X=0, Y=0, Z=0)

Let me trace through the first example:
- N = 5
- A: {1, 2, 3} (people 1, 2, 3 belong to A)
- B: {4, 5} (people 4, 5 belong to B)
- C: {3, 4} (people 3, 4 own product C)

Condition 1: ¬A ∩ C = people not in A AND in C
- Not in A: {4, 5}
- In C: {3, 4}
- Intersection: {4} (only person 4 is in both)

Condition 2: B ∩ C = people in B AND in C
- In B: {4, 5}
- In C: {3, 4}
- Intersection: {4} (only person 4 is in both)

Union: {4} ∪ {4} = {4}
Count: 1 ✓

This matches the expected output.

```python
# Read input
N = int(input())

# Read organization A
line_A = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_A[0]
if X > 0:
    A = set(line_A[1:])
else:
    A = set()

# Read organization B
line_B = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_B[0]
if Y > 0:
    B = set(line_B[1:])
else:
    B = set()

# Read product C
line_C = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_C[0]
if Z > 0:
    C = set(line_C[1:])
else:
    C = set()

# Find people who meet condition 1: not in A AND in C
condition1 = C - A  # This is (¬A ∩ C)

# Find people who meet condition 2: in B AND in C
condition2 = B & C  # This is (B ∩ C)

# Find union of both conditions
result = condition1 | condition2

# Output the count
print(len(result))
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. People who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. People who belong to organization B AND own product C

This is essentially finding the size of the union of two sets: `(¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)`

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Understand the sets
- Set A: people who belong to organization A
- Set B: people who belong to organization B  
- Set C: people who own product C
- ¬A: people who do NOT belong to organization A (complement of A)

### Step 2: Identify what we need
We need to count people who satisfy either:
- Not in A AND in C
- In B AND in C

This is equivalent to: `(C - A) ∪ (B ∩ C)` where `C - A` means people in C but not in A.

### Step 3: Algorithm
1. Read input values
2. Create sets for A, B, and C
3. Find people who are in C but not in A (¬A ∩ C)
4. Find people who are in B and in C (B ∩ C)
5. Combine these two sets and count the total unique people

### Step 4: Implementation

```python
# Read input
N = int(input())

# Read organization A
line_a = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_a[0]
if X > 0:
    A = set(line_a[1:])
else:
    A = set()

# Read organization B
line_b = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_b[0]
if Y > 0:
    B = set(line_b[1:])
else:
    B = set()

# Read product C owners
line_c = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_c[0]
if Z > 0:
    C = set(line_c[1:])
else:
    C = set()

# Find people who meet condition 1: not in A and in C
condition1 = C - A  # This is ¬A ∩ C

# Find people who meet condition 2: in B and in C
condition2 = B & C  # This is B ∩ C

# Combine both conditions and count unique people
result = condition1 | condition2  # Union of both sets

# Output the count
print(len(result))
```

## Verification with examples

**Example 1:**
- N = 5
- A = {1, 2, 3}
- B = {4, 5}
- C = {3, 4}

Condition 1: Not in A AND in C = {3, 4} - {1, 2, 3} = {4}
Condition 2: In B AND in C = {4, 5} ∩ {3, 4} = {4}
Union = {4}
Count = 1 ✓

**Example 2:**
- N = 100
- A = {1, 4, 100}
- B = {} (empty)
- C = {2, 3}

Condition 1: Not in A AND in C = {2, 3} - {1, 4, 100} = {2, 3}
Condition 2: In B AND in C = {} ∩ {2, 3} = {}
Union = {2, 3}
Count = 2 ✓

The solution works correctly for both examples!

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. People who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. People who belong to organization B AND own product C

This is the union of two sets: (¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)

Where:
- ¬A is the complement of set A (people not in A)
- C is the set of people who own product C
- We need to count people who satisfy either condition (without double counting)

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Parse the input**: Read N, then the three sets (A, B, C)
2. **Convert to sets**: Store the membership in sets for easy operations
3. **Calculate the two conditions**:
   - Condition 1: People not in A AND in C → (¬A ∩ C)
   - Condition 2: People in B AND in C → (B ∩ C)
4. **Union of both conditions**: People who satisfy either condition
5. **Return the count**

Let me trace through the examples:

**Example 1:**
- N = 5
- A = {1, 2, 3} (people 1, 2, 3 belong to A)
- B = {4, 5} (people 4, 5 belong to B)
- C = {3, 4} (people 3, 4 own product C)

Condition 1: Not in A AND in C = {4, 5} ∩ {3, 4} = {4}
Condition 2: In B AND in C = {4, 5} ∩ {3, 4} = {4}
Union = {4}, count = 1 ✓

**Example 2:**
- N = 100
- A = {1, 4, 100}
- B = {} (empty)
- C = {2, 3}

Condition 1: Not in A AND in C = (all except {1,4,100}) ∩ {2,3} = {2,3}
Condition 2: In B AND in C = {} ∩ {2,3} = {}
Union = {2,3}, count = 2 ✓

## Implementation

```python
# Read input
N = int(input())

# Read organization A
line_a = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_a[0]
if X > 0:
    A = set(line_a[1:])
else:
    A = set()

# Read organization B
line_b = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_b[0]
if Y > 0:
    B = set(line_b[1:])
else:
    B = set()

# Read product C
line_c = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_c[0]
if Z > 0:
    C = set(line_c[1:])
else:
    C = set()

# Calculate condition 1: not in A and in C
not_A = set(range(1, N + 1)) - A
condition1 = not_A.intersection(C)

# Calculate condition 2: in B and in C
condition2 = B.intersection(C)

# Union of both conditions
result = condition1.union(condition2)

# Output the count
print(len(result))
```

The solution works by:
1. Reading all inputs and storing them as sets
2. Finding people not in A (complement of A) and intersecting with C
3. Finding people in B and intersecting with C
4. Taking the union of both sets to avoid double counting
5. Returning the size of the resulting set

Time complexity: O(N + X + Y + Z) which is efficient for the given constraints.

Submitted Code

View Extracted Code 
# Read input
N = int(input())

# Read organization A
line_a = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_a[0]
if X > 0:
    A = set(line_a[1:])
else:
    A = set()

# Read organization B
line_b = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_b[0]
if Y > 0:
    B = set(line_b[1:])
else:
    B = set()

# Read product C
line_c = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_c[0]
if Z > 0:
    C = set(line_c[1:])
else:
    C = set()

# Calculate condition 1: not in A and in C
not_A = set(range(1, N + 1)) - A
condition1 = not_A.intersection(C)

# Calculate condition 2: in B and in C
condition2 = B.intersection(C)

# Union of both conditions
result = condition1.union(condition2)

# Output the count
print(len(result))

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

The secret organization AiZu AnalyticS has launched a top-secret investigation. There are N people targeted, with identification numbers from 1 to N. As an AZAS Information Strategy Investigator, you have decided to determine the number of people in your target who meet at least one of the following conditions:

* Those who do not belong to the organization $ A $ and who own the product $ C $.
* A person who belongs to the organization $ B $ and owns the product $ C $.



A program that calculates the number of people who meet the conditions when the identification number of the person who belongs to the organization $ A $, the person who belongs to the organization $ B $, and the person who owns the product $ C $ is given as input. Create. However, be careful not to count duplicate people who meet both conditions.

(Supplement: Regarding the above conditions)
Let $ A $, $ B $, and $ C $ be the sets of some elements selected from the set of natural numbers from 1 to $ N $. The number of people who satisfy the condition is the number of elements that satisfy $ (\ bar {A} \ cap C) \ cup (B \ cap C) $ (painted part in the figure). However, $ \ bar {A} $ is a complement of the set $ A $.

On the third line, the number Y (0 ≤ Y ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ B $, followed by the identification number bi (1 ≤ bi ≤ N) of those who belong to the organization $ B $. Given. Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of people who meet at least one of these conditions:
1. People who do NOT belong to organization A AND own product C
2. People who belong to organization B AND own product C

This is equivalent to finding the size of the union of two sets:
- (Complement of A) ∩ C
- B ∩ C

Using set theory: |(¬A ∩ C) ∪ (B ∩ C)| = |(¬A ∩ C)| + |(B ∩ C)| - |(¬A ∩ C) ∩ (B ∩ C)|

But we can also think of it more simply: we want people who own product C AND either:
- Don't belong to A, OR
- Belong to B

This can be rewritten as: people who own product C AND (don't belong to A OR belong to B)

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read input values
2. Store the sets of people belonging to organization A, organization B, and owning product C
3. Find people who own product C and either:
   - Don't belong to A, OR
   - Belong to B
4. Count the total number of such people

Let me trace through the first example:
- N = 5
- A: people {1, 2, 3}
- B: people {4, 5}
- C: people {3, 4}

People who own product C: {3, 4}

For person 3:
- Belongs to A? Yes (3 ∈ {1,2,3})
- Belongs to B? No (3 ∉ {4,5})
- Condition 1: Not in A and owns C? No (3 is in A)
- Condition 2: In B and owns C? No (3 is not in B)
- So person 3 doesn't satisfy any condition

For person 4:
- Belongs to A? No (4 ∉ {1,2,3})
- Belongs to B? Yes (4 ∈ {4,5})
- Condition 1: Not in A and owns C? Yes (4 is not in A and owns C)
- Condition 2: In B and owns C? Yes (4 is in B and owns C)
- So person 4 satisfies both conditions, but should be counted only once

So total = 1 (only person 4)

## Implementation

```python
# Read input
N = int(input())

# Read organization A
line_A = list(map(int, input().split()))
X = line_A[0]
if X > 0:
    A = set(line_A[1:])
else:
    A = set()

# Read organization B
line_B = list(map(int, input().split()))
Y = line_B[0]
if Y > 0:
    B = set(line_B[1:])
else:
    B = set()

# Read product C owners
line_C = list(map(int, input().split()))
Z = line_C[0]
if Z > 0:
    C = set(line_C[1:])
else:
    C = set()

# Find people who meet at least one condition
# Condition 1: Not in A AND in C
# Condition 2: In B AND in C
# We want the union of these two sets

not_A_in_C = C - A  # People who own C but are not in A
B_in_C = B & C      # People who are in B and own C

# Union of both sets
result = len(not_A_in_C | B_in_C)

print(result)
```

The key insight is that we don't need to worry about duplicates because we're using set union, which automatically handles duplicates (each person is only counted once). 

In the first example:
- C = {3, 4}
- A = {1, 2, 3}, so C - A = {4} (only 4 is in C but not in A)
- B = {4, 5}, so B ∩ C = {4} 
- Union = {4} → size = 1 ✓

In the second example:
- N = 100
- A = {1, 4, 100}
- B = {} (empty)
- C = {2, 3}
- C - A = {2, 3} (since neither 2 nor 3 is in A)
- B ∩ C = {} (since B is empty)
- Union = {2, 3} → size = 2 ✓

The solution works correctly for both examples.
